# vhttpd Refactor Action Plan

This plan turns the prioritized findings in [`CODE_REVIEW_2026_08_06.md`](CODE_REVIEW_2026_08_06.md) §4 into a concrete, sequenced work list. Each milestone lists its scope, the files it touches, the verification signal, and the rollback strategy.

Track this file as milestones complete; cross-link any deviation back to the review.

## M5 CI 矩阵收尾 — 2026-10-22

当前候选 `fd275acf9d5592a8037ed422509211acdce36345`：用户已明确批准发布必要 V2/M4 基线，334 个 V 测试对应的既有运行时、PHP SDK、CodexBot 示例、协议/E2E 夹具、Makefile 和安全脚本已单独提交。排除 `desktop/**`、视觉 UI 和知识库元数据，仅带入完整 E2E 所需的共享 `admin/ui/relay-config.js`。原工作区暂存状态保留，无新增局部审查。[PR 三平台检查](https://github.com/guweigang/vhttpd/actions/runs/39706839539) 与 [nightly 同构全量矩阵](https://github.com/guweigang/vhttpd/actions/runs/39706928127) 均已启动，结果未完成，不核销远端验收。

首个 Linux 失败已证实为 CI 依赖平台选择错误：macOS ARM TCC 包被用于全部平台，触发 builtin GC 链接失败。已改为 Linux amd64/macOS Intel/macOS ARM 各自固定提交；[原失败 artifact](https://github.com/guweigang/vhttpd/actions/runs/39705799202/artifacts/12394408740) 和 job 原始日志已保存。旧提交重复任务已取消，不拼接旧候选结果。

远端验收首轮记录（已被后续基线提交取代）：8 个 CI 文件已隔离提交并推送至 `codex/m5-ci-acceptance`，初始提交 `c592fc3d97471e92df1de66ad611fedbe61e8951`；[验收 PR #21](https://github.com/guweigang/vhttpd/pull/21) 以 `refactor/v2` 为基线，[首轮 PR 三平台检查](https://github.com/guweigang/vhttpd/actions/runs/39705520231) 已启动。新 nightly 尚未进入默认分支时，可通过已注册的 binaries workflow 的 `validation=nightly` 手动入口调用相同 reusable 全量矩阵，不提前合并默认分支。当前工作区另有大量未提交 V2/M4 运行时及测试改动，是否纳入必要基线已向用户确认；初始 PR 仅含 CI 文件，其结果不得冒称整个本地候选的验收。原工作区暂存状态保留，桌面/UI/知识库元数据未发布。证据目录 `tmp/m5-remote-validation/`；最终结果待同一提交的 PR 与全量矩阵完成后核销。

**M5 配置与本地验证已完成，远端验收尚未关闭。** 三平台 PR 检查新增 inproc/CodexBot fast，保留 fast → 生产构建 → 显式使用本次产物的 E2E；nightly 于 03:00 UTC 跑三平台 V 全量（含 lifecycle）、PHP package/Pest、生产构建和 E2E，失败也上传日志。V/vjsx/QuickJS 与 bootstrap 固定提交并携带已验收的本地补丁，action 固定 SHA，Node/Python/Composer 固定完整版本，PHP 固定 8.4 系列并记录实际补丁版本和系统库版本。

新获取并重建的工具链通过 inproc 6/6、CodexBot fast 6/6；PHP package 10/10、Pest 79 项/557 断言、actionlint/shellcheck 与矩阵配置检查通过。证据为 `tmp/m5-ci-validation/`，细节及远端完成标准见下方 Milestone 5。**V2 剩余为 M5 远端 PR/nightly 证据、Linux 验收与 M7；不再把 M5 CI 配置列为尚未实现。** 本轮未扩展局部审查，也未宣称重跑 334 个 V 全量或生产 E2E。

## 前轮 M4 日志统一收尾 — 2026-10-22

当前范围仅为 M4。已移除 `runtime_trace` 和硬编码 trace 文件，业务事件直接进入统一 emit；WebSocket 的原 `ws.*` 诊断经显式注入的 emit 能力记录为 `runtime.trace`，保留 label 与调用点字段。未新增独立 `runtime_trace_path`，避免重建第二条路径；日志说明更新在现有 [日志系统文档](../docs/开发指南/调试技巧和工具/日志系统.md)，不创建已经迁移掉的 `docs/OVERVIEW.md`。这是对原 M4 路径提案的明确调整，业务事件名、NDJSON 字段、统计、默认文本输出和生产 WARN 契约保持不变。新增 `VHTTPD_LOG_FORMAT=json` 的控制台格式，event log 写失败显式报 error，空路径显式禁用。

**M4 已完成实现及本轮 macOS 验收。** 最终证据位于 `tmp/m4-logging-validation/`，9,747 个输入文件，清单 SHA-256 `b3bd410575557e93b99e632dfc3eb4eaac856a8c273bb55e8d6a7916001bcedf`。logging/ws 15/15、相关运行时 4/4、生产模式日志回归 1/1、安全检查均通过；真正 `-prod`、DB/OpenSSL/QuickJS、boehm 生产产物为 10,742,064 字节，SHA-256 `bb5a3398113d82c55eabbadb91a25417cb62114bdd0d96b17723c459b4f3f304`。完整生产 E2E 已通过原入口：新增三项日志契约、配置验收 205 项/0 失败、CLI 4 项和其余前置检查均通过。补充黑盒验证默认 WARN、event log 写失败 error、健康请求与 SIGTERM 143 通过。

JSON 运行日志的通道为 stderr，框架 stdout 启动提示保留文本。候选 C 首次新测试误将两者合并解析，已只修正测试通道假设，同时验证 stdout 原提示；生产 V 源码、V 测试输入和二进制完全未变（`production-equivalence.json`），不是拼接不同生产版本的绿测。之后完整 E2E 曾出现一次外部创建凭证的 3 秒请求超时；原样独立回归及完整入口复跑均通过，未改容量、断言或超时，原因尚未确认，失败保留于 `attempt-d-timeout/`。准备轮 `attempt-a/`、`attempt-b/` 与 C 失败现场均保留，不作为验收通过证据。

最终源码、工作区源码、冻结 V/vjsx/QuickJS、原生运行时校验均无漂移；原工作区二进制保留，源码中已无 `runtime_trace` 与硬编码 trace 路径。日志文档已更新。上一轮 E 的 332/332 仍是旧候选的全量证据，本轮只执行用户要求的相关回归和完整生产 E2E，不冒称重跑整个 V 矩阵。Linux 命中既存跟踪修改停止规则，本轮只读检查，未构建或测试。**V2 剩余阻塞为 Linux、M5 CI 扩展与 M7 V1 弃用/默认配置；M4 不再列为实现缺口。**

## 前轮 V2 验收阻塞修复与复验 — 2026-10-21～22（候选 E）

**前轮状态（2026-10-22，候选 E）：本轮测试夹具、PHP SDK 自动加载、生产日志断言和输入完整性阻塞已解除；同一冻结候选 E 的 macOS 完整矩阵通过。V2 整体验收仍未关闭，剩余 Linux 与 M4/M5/M7。** 范围限于测试夹具、PHP SDK 自动加载与生产日志断言，不继续扩展局部生命周期审查。下方“首轮”保留失败现场，不能与本轮结果混用。

**前轮候选 E（2026-10-22）：** `tmp/v2-closeout-acceptance-validation/snapshot/`，9,743 个输入文件，清单 SHA-256 `dcc5e3ab9f21c8f7f0a95ea56c07faa4774e6deb25301832fd2508cde4184b58`。沿用冻结 V/vjsx/QuickJS，PHP、Node、Python 采用已校验哈希的固定路径。D 的完整 all 已收集：327/332 通过、5 个失败、无未执行文件，耗时约 5650 秒；源码与外部依赖无漂移，原二进制保留。D 的 PHP 包 10 个脚本、应用 79 个用例（557 条断言）、安全检查、生产构建与完整生产 E2E 均通过，但不能据此把 D 的整体验收标绿。

E 只集中修复 D 暴露的五处测试问题：MCP sampling 夹具改用正式会话初始化和 capability API，避免零时间戳先被 TTL 回收；provider registry 的两项无 DB 编译断言加相应构建条件，并额外执行该文件 `WITH_DB=0`；runtime context 测试使用现有 Relay 绑定接口，检查未注册目标拒绝与已绑定目标入队；slot request lease 和 worker lifecycle event 的 `sleep`/异进程监听假夹具改为真实子进程持有 Unix socket。原策略、旧代完成、新代计数、事件重入和失败清理断言保留，未修改生产生命周期逻辑。

E 的正式复验已全部结束：五处定向回归及 provider registry 的无 DB 分支通过；PHP 包 10 个脚本、Pest 79 个用例/557 条断言、安全检查、真正 `-prod` 构建与完整生产 E2E 均通过。正式 `make test-all` 为 332/332 文件通过、0 失败、0 未执行，耗时 4899.5 秒。按用户选择，以全量逐文件结果核销 fast 318/318、inproc 6/6、CodexBot fast 6/6、lifecycle 1/1；各入口编译参数一致，未重复执行包装入口，不把覆盖结论写成独立命令已执行。DB 事务租约文件也在 all 中通过。生产产物 10,758,096 字节，SHA-256 `0d6eeb4f79902490413da36e13ad8374c7b3f222c7cead0315fced78e3a30e02`；完整 E2E 通过原入口，配置验收 205 项、FAIL 0，CLI 4 项与全部前置协议/PHP/进程/桥接检查通过。Live DB/安装态 WordPress 未启用，不以默认 smoke 冒充这些环境验收。

最终证据位于 `tmp/v2-closeout-acceptance-validation/`：`matrix-results.json`、`coverage-observed.json`、`entrypoint-coverage-equivalence.json` 记录实际命令、退出码与逐家族覆盖；`integrity-final.json`、`native-integrity-final.json`、`root-source-integrity-final.json` 确认候选源码、冻结 V/vjsx/QuickJS、PHP/Node/Python/编译工具均无漂移，当前工作区源码与候选一致，原工作区二进制未替换。历史 A/B/C/D 失败保留，不与 E 拼接。

| 验收项 | 本轮核销 | 剩余完成标准 |
|---|---|---|
| B1 V 夹具/构建条件 | 已解除：正式 all 332/332，DB 开/关分支均验证 | 本机矩阵完成；跨平台仍见 B3 |
| B2 PHP SDK/自动加载 | 已解除：真实 SDK、Composer/源码入口、10 脚本及 Pest 79/557 通过 | 本轮无剩余实现项 |
| B4 生产日志断言 | 已解除：显式 INFO 的原断言在 E 生产版完整 E2E 通过 | 生产默认 WARN 保持原契约 |
| R1 输入完整性 | 已解除：源码/外部源码快照及原生运行时前后校验一致 | 后续更改须按新候选重新验证 |
| M0 | 当前候选身份与测试证据齐全；重构前基线缺失保持可见 | 首轮性能数字只描述首轮产物，未宣称 E 性能改善 |

| B3 Linux | 未执行构建/测试；只读复查确认既存跟踪修改，V 为 clean `local-dev`、0.5.2 4f3082f | 按技能要求取得可执行工作区状态，再验证同版本；不重置既存修改 |

V2 关闭条件仍为：上述同版本矩阵证据有效，Linux 阻塞解除，M4/M5/M7 完成或有明确的范围调整决定。本轮未扩展局部生命周期审查，也未修改生产生命周期逻辑。

- B1：将跨文件共用的 admin draft、HTTP worker session、config changeset、build directory 辅助函数移入对应的 `*_test_support.v`；原测试文件、用例和断言保留。未合并测试入口或排除文件。
- B2：补齐 [仓库内 Upstream SDK](../php/package/src/VHttpd/Upstream/WebSocket/EventRouter.php)的真实事件路由、命令容器及序列化实现；[源码自动加载](../php/package/autoload.php)、[应用加载入口](../examples/codexbot-app/autoload.php)与 worker fallback 使用同一套 SDK。新增包回归验证事件分发、命令顺序和已捕获凭证。应用测试的旧重启命令计数现明确检查清理命令及 generation；取消测试在跨连接写入前关闭 SQLite 读取游标，保留取消和迟到事件的原断言。定向 Pest 79/79、557 条断言通过。
- B4：原因确认是 [生产日志默认级别](../src/logging/runtime_logger.v)为 WARN，而 E2E 断言 INFO 请求日志。metrics/Relay 的子进程与 config acceptance 显式设置 `VHTTPD_LOG_LEVEL=info`；生产默认级别、原断言及超时不变。同一首轮生产二进制的定向复测 11/11 通过，不能替代新候选的完整生产 E2E。
- R1：本轮在执行正式矩阵前复制 V 编译器、V 源码、vjsx 与 QuickJS；复制的 vjsx 模块链接已指向冻结副本。运行前逐文件哈希、提交和本地修改清单保存在 `dependency-inputs.json`，PHP Composer vendor 也包含于应用快照。结果必须与运行后的输入完整性校验一起判定。

新证据目录为 `tmp/v2-closeout-fix-validation/`。应用快照排除 Pest 执行结果缓存后的输入数量与精确清单哈希见 `identity.json`；初始含缓存的清单保留在 `source-manifest-with-generated-pest-cache.json`，该修订只排除运行时产物，生产和测试源码未变化；原二进制保留。正式矩阵使用冻结 PATH、VEXE、VMODULES、vjsx 与 QuickJS，VJOBS=2，取消 fail-fast；逐目标命令、时间和退出码写入 `matrix-results.json`。

候选 A 的 PHP 包 10 个脚本、PHP 应用 79 个用例（557 条断言）、安全检查与生产构建通过；其完整 E2E 已通过 metrics/Relay 日志断言，但在 WordPress worker 启动处暴露本轮 source loader 与 Composer 重复加载 runtime 的问题，完整错误及产物归档于 `attempt-a/`，不计为完整 E2E 通过。已修复 Composer 优先发现与兼容入口防重复加载，并增加无 Composer 的两份真实 SDK 共存回归；WordPress worker 已独立启动成功。当前候选 B 已重新冻结，输入清单 SHA-256 `26c01176bc37a14308efc59a3ca3395070f36cae6744f3dda66681be44b7a839`，正式矩阵从头执行，不混用候选 A 的结果。候选 B 的生产构建重新通过，二进制 SHA-256 仍为 `51c6474eb776eead0d9f3123e3ad02493dc916bec64fe5d81534367ce3a51011`（10,758,096 字节）。完整生产 E2E 已通过，原 `run.sh` 全流程成功，配置验收 205 项、FAIL 0，CLI 4 项及所有前置 Node/PHP/协议/进程/桥接用例通过；原日志断言和超时保持不变。B2、B4 已在该候选复验通过。B 的正式 V all 已通过原先失败的 `admin_draft_publication_owner_test.v`，并继续发现 `config_publication_owner_test.v` 将另一个测试文件中的 `config_check_ok` 当作回调值引用；调用括号扫描遗漏了这种引用。已在工作区移入共享支持文件，单文件正式入口编译及执行通过，冻结 B 保持不变以收集完整结果；完成收集后再集中冻结最终候选复验，不能把这次单文件补测混作 B 全量通过。整体 V all/default/inproc/CodexBot 矩阵仍待完成。Linux 只读预检已成功返回，挂载仓库仍有本轮之前的跟踪变更，技能停止规则继续适用；未执行 Linux 构建或环境修改。M4/M5/M7 的实际缺口保持原状态，不以本轮修复自动核销。

## 首轮 V2 收尾验收 — 2026-10-21（历史结果）

**当前结论：收尾验收未通过，V2 尚不能关闭。** 本轮冻结源码快照，核销历史计划与执行完整矩阵，不再扩展新的局部生命周期审查。后续的历史进度段落保留其当日语境；本节汇总当前状态，旧段落中的 pending 不应直接当作今日状态，也不能用历史局部绿测替代本轮整体验收。

| 里程碑 | 本轮核对 | 核销依据与仍需满足的条件 |
|---|---|---|
| M0 基线 | 历史证据不全，当前基线已补录 | 未找到 `pre-refactor-baseline` 标签；不能把当前提交伪装成重构前基线。记录本轮源码、工具链、构建与测试结果；缺少历史数据时不宣称性能改善。 |
| M1 unsafe bridge | 实现目标已完成，整体验证待收口 | [类型化 App 适配](../src/app_executor_facade.v)与[安全检查](../scripts/lint_safety.sh)替代旧包装器；原先改成值快照的策略已由后文记录的 live capability 方案替代，不重复改写。仍需本轮矩阵与跨平台结果。 |
| M2 Executor/worker 拆分 | 实现目标已完成，整体验证待收口 | [WorkerState](../src/worker/types.v)、[引擎编排](../src/engine_runtime.v)与[应用适配](../src/app_worker_backend.v)分离状态归属和应用装配；以矩阵验证现有实现，不再按旧文件名做机械搬迁。 |
| M3 command/Feishu 拆分 | 实现目标已完成，整体验证待收口 | [命令能力接口](../src/command_host.v)、[Feishu 状态](../src/feishu/types.v)和[桥接能力接口](../src/feishu_card_bridge_host.v)已显式注入；近期发布、重放、背压及停止修复已落地，完整矩阵待本轮确认。 |

| M5 CI 矩阵 | 部分完成 | [现有 workflow](../.github/workflows/vhttpd-binaries.yml)已有 PR fast/prod/E2E；未找到计划要求的 inproc/CodexBot PR 步骤及 nightly-full workflow，也没有本冻结版本的远端 CI 运行证据。[包同步](../.github/workflows/sync-vphp-package.yml)只发布 `php/package` 子树，`php/app` 排除与当前发布范围一致，不为无关应用变更触发包发布。 |
| M6 可取消退避 | 实现目标已完成，整体验证待收口 | [会话取消与等待](../src/connection_runtime/owner.c.v)、[上游连接循环](../src/ws/upstream_runtime.v)及[桥接客户端](../src/feishu_card_bridge_client_runtime.v)以会话归属停止；短轮询保留，不能把所有 sleep 的存在都误当未实现。 |
| M7 V1 弃用与默认示例 | 未完成 | [默认配置](../vhttpd.toml)仍是 V1 布局；[兼容投影](../src/config/v1_plan_compat.v)仍在原路径。原提案的移除日期不等于已发布承诺，仍需完成或明确批准调整范围后才能关闭。 |

本轮候选快照位于 `tmp/v2-closeout-validation/snapshot/`；清单含 332 个 V 测试文件、2157 个直接声明的测试函数。`identity.json`、`source-manifest.json`、`test-inventory.json` 记录精确版本，不能仅用持续变化的 autosave HEAD 代表测试输入。所有矩阵目标使用该快照；测试失败后保留该目标的未执行部分，不能标绿。当前证据集中在 `tmp/v2-closeout-validation/`。外部 V/vjsx 的 HEAD 与最初记录相同，但两个工作区含本地修改，本轮开始时未记录这些修改的文件哈希；`toolchain-final.json` 只能描述最终状态，不能证明整个运行期间外部依赖无漂移。应用快照无漂移的结论不覆盖这些外部输入；下一次可接受候选须一并冻结或预先记录它们。

**完成标准：** 同一冻结源码的正式测试矩阵与生产构建通过，生产二进制的完整 E2E 通过；跨平台验收阻塞消除；M4/M5/M7 的实际缺口完成，或由负责人明确接受移出本轮；历史基线缺失保持可见，当前指标不用于虚构前后提升。只有这些条件满足，才可把本节改为验收完成。Linux 预检目前命中技能的既存跟踪变更停止规则，未执行 Linux 构建、测试或环境修改。Live DB/已安装 WordPress 与长期负载是独立环境验收，默认 smoke 通过不代表已覆盖这些场景。

跨目标去重后，本轮共观察到 20 个 V 测试文件通过、1 个编译失败，另 311 个文件没有最终执行结果；逐文件名单见 `coverage-observed.json`。这包含从未完成的 fast 入口取得的 route 单文件结果与独立补测，不能把它写成全量目标已完成。所有已启动的最终测试目标均已结束，本轮没有为修复门禁而改动生产或测试源码。下一步限定为解除 B1/B2/B4、固定完整输入并重跑验收，处理 Linux 及 M4/M5/M7 的完成或明确延期决定，不再扩大局部安全审查。

### 本轮生产构建与当前基线

同一冻结源码的 `make prod WITH_DB=1 VPHP_V_GC=boehm` 已通过，OpenSSL/QuickJS 开启；二进制 SHA-256 为 `70b1400e4a5fa5add5fcb3c66395bea014e7da36f2aa07362df28e6d4d13ad6c`，10,741,584 字节。当前 arm64 主机的最小健康检查配置测得空闲 RSS 三次均为 24,416 KiB，20 次预热后 100 次单客户端顺序 `/health` 请求 p50 1.342 ms、p99 7.857 ms。每次请求新建连接；其他编译任务可能仍在运行，因此这些数值仅为本轮配置基线，不是性能门槛、吞吐测试或重构前后改善证据。构建墙钟约 1253 秒，由新建日志到链接产物时间估算，不冒充 `/usr/bin/time` 精确测量。精确方法和样本摘要见 `tmp/v2-closeout-validation/current-baseline.json`。

第一次生产 E2E 的 Admin UI 404 来自快照遗漏 `admin/ui` 运行时资源；补齐 18 个运行时资源文件后保持二进制、生产源码和测试源码不变，从冻结目录重新运行。初始清单与补齐清单均保留；该诊断不能列为已确认产品缺陷。最终清单共 9,652 个文件，SHA-256 `3b24fa3618d592e1a441fb048bfb5e65a97463a4b1bdd050d071f266e6267c38`，校验无漂移；根目录原有二进制未替换。

完整生产 E2E 已执行并失败：协议、进程生命周期、Admin 登录、可信签发/重放、跨宿主撤销/轮换/队列、sent/update 与 PHP/TS 一次性回复检查通过，随后 metrics 日志断言失败。原入口后续未到达的步骤已单独补跑：Relay UI/config/cross-include、PHP stream 示例和 CLI 4 项通过；Relay 身份与 config acceptance 在 B4 所列日志断言失败。配置验收在停止前通过 89 项，后续未执行部分不能核销。独立 metrics 复测同样失败。没有放宽原超时、删除断言或修改 `run.sh`；不能将这些补跑拼成“完整 E2E 通过”。完整矩阵及原始日志索引见 `tmp/v2-closeout-validation/matrix-results.json` 和 `report.txt`。

## Progress — 2026-10-14

The V3 compatibility baseline is established. The first Milestone 1 slice removes the unsafe `AppFacadeWrapper`: `App` now implements `executor.AppFacade` directly in `src/app_executor_facade.v`, while transitional executor aliases and factory helpers live in `src/executor_types.v`. `src/executor_bridge.v` is deleted. The two new facade regressions verify that configuration updates remain visible and mutations affect the original App rather than a boxed copy. `make lint-safety` runs before `test-fast` and rejects raw App casts in the executor facade; provider callback casts remain a separate migration.

**Deviation from the original Milestone 1 plan:** V3 compiles the direct typed interface successfully, so the current slice preserves the existing live-reference capability contracts instead of replacing them with snapshots. This avoids stale configuration during runtime plan replacement. The original [review finding 2.2](CODE_REVIEW_2026_08_06.md) describes the removed workaround. Splitting the remaining broad AppFacade dependency into subsystem-owned capabilities, full-suite validation, and Linux/production validation are still pending; Milestone 1 is not closed.

Validation for this slice: strict V3 build and 206 end-to-end checks pass on macOS (4 CLI checks and 202 config/runtime checks). The facade, executor, and worker integration suites also pass (3 test files, including both new live-reference regressions). `git diff --check` and `make lint-safety` pass.

### Milestone 2 worker ownership slice — 2026-10-14

Pool selection, queue accounting, process ensure/restart, request accounting, drain/resume, and selection diagnostics now live under `src/worker/` as WorkerState operations. Connection retries and stream/MCP/WebSocket transport dispatch also live in that module with explicit callback contexts. `worker.LifecyclePort` owns the event dependency with a safe no-op default; connector and dispatch callbacks are required at construction.

`EngineRuntime` retains primary/named-pool routing. `src/app_worker_backend.v` collects application adapters and supplies callbacks for events, pool selection, and request completion. Three unused App helpers were removed. `worker.DrainStatus` is shared by worker administration and runtime-plan replacement, with the existing response fields preserved. The core module references neither App nor EngineRuntime; `make lint-safety` now enforces this boundary. Existing mutex acquisition order, queue error classes, and request/release accounting were preserved.

Validation: strict V3 build with DB enabled passes on macOS; 4 integration test files and 3 standalone worker test files pass. New regressions cover concurrent exclusive leasing, busy/draining worker exclusion, drain/resume counters, and immediate queue backpressure. All 206 E2E checks pass (4 CLI plus 202 config/runtime checks), including controlled queue-full, queued completion, timeout, and replacement scenarios. Full-suite and Linux validation remain pending; the VM compiler is unchanged and the mounted checkout contains pre-existing unrelated tracked changes, so the Linux testing skill's preflight stop rule applies.

### Engine administration and executor capabilities — 2026-10-14

WorkerState now owns restart-by-id/restart-all, process and pool snapshots, queue metrics, backend lifecycle context construction/start/stop, and scheme/environment mutation. `src/worker/admin.v`, `metrics.v`, and `backend_lifecycle.v` contain these operations without an admin or application dependency. `EngineRuntime` delegates per-pool operations and retains pool selection and executor orchestration. The application layer projects worker snapshots into the existing admin response types, preserving JSON fields and restart error strings.

The five worker facade ports now store and accept only their corresponding capability interfaces: backend config, sockets, stream, MCP, and WebSocket dispatch. They no longer retain a complete AppFacade. SocketWorkerExecutor and PhpCgiExecutor keep their polymorphic AppFacade entry points as adapters; their HTTP algorithms and the socket WebSocket-open algorithm live in `src/executor/worker_dispatch_core.v`, accepting only WorkerSocketFacade and WorkerBackendConfigFacade. Embedded executor host paths still use the broader facade and remain future work.

Validation: strict V3 build with DB enabled, 8 worker/executor module test files, 4 integration test files, and all 206 E2E checks pass on macOS. New tests exercise hosts implementing only a single capability, live mutation through narrow ports, both HTTP cores without unrelated App capabilities, detached admin snapshots, restart errors, metrics, and lifecycle configuration isolation. Safety lint now prevents complete AppFacade references in worker ports/dispatch core. Full-suite and Linux validation remain pending under the previously recorded VM preflight stop rule.

### Registry lookup and embedded host boundary — 2026-10-14

`src/engine_registry.v` provides a single `lookup_engine` entry point with explicit worker-config, strict executor-kind, dispatch-pool, and drain-pool policies. Configuration getters, socket selection, HTTP kind dispatch, and drain/resume all use it. Existing behavior is preserved: dispatch pools prefer a named key and fall back to primary; worker config gives the primary kind precedence; strict HTTP kind lookup does not add empty-key aliases; drain additionally accepts empty/main/primary aliases. Lookup results retain the original WorkerState and distinguish primary identity from a named pool literally called `main`.

Embedded executor lane state, tasks, request contexts, host APIs, plugins, snapshot/startup paths, and dispatch internals now use `executor.EmbeddedHost`. It composes only runtime configuration, platform events/snapshots, provider bridge, command envelopes, and WebSocket command execution. It has no worker socket/configuration or transport dispatch methods. Seven uniform LogicExecutor entry points keep AppFacade only in `inproc_vjsx_facade_adapter.v`, adapting it to EmbeddedHost once. The internal host and lane references remain live across configuration updates; a JavaScript HTTP test verifies this using a host that implements no worker capabilities. Safety lint prevents broad AppFacade references outside the embedded adapter.

Validation: strict V3 build, 4 executor module test files, 5 registry/worker/executor integration files, and 3 embedded runtime test files pass on macOS. The last group includes all 70 inproc executor test functions plus host API and startup coverage. All 206 E2E checks pass on an isolated rerun. The first E2E run overlapped heavy compilation and missed the provider WebSocket probe's 10-second deadline; it passed with the same binary after that load ended. A pre-existing snapshot assertion assumed JSON object key order; it now decodes the snapshot and asserts the HTTP capability value semantically.

### Optional embedded request-host lookup — 2026-10-14

`src/executor/inproc_vjsx_request_host.v` centralizes host lookup with explicit request-only, lane fallback, and executor fallback policies. It captures the host and request metadata under one state lock and releases that lock before invoking host services. Request context, lane-retained host, and executor-retained host now store `?EmbeddedHost`; empty and destroyed hosts use `none`. Nine unsafe interface nil placeholders are removed from config, emit, bridge/WebSocket dispatch, snapshot, affinity, and actor paths. Request-only APIs stop resolving after request completion; asynchronous WebSocket dispatch retains its lane host without expired trace metadata, while snapshot retains its executor fallback.

Validation: strict V3 build with DB enabled, 4 executor module test files, and 3 embedded integration test files pass on macOS (including all 70 inproc executor functions). New lookup and real JavaScript callback tests cover absent hosts, invalid lane indexes, request precedence, metadata normalization, live host mutations, request clearing, lane destruction, and fallback behavior. Safety lint guards against unsafe host interface placeholders; `git diff --check` passes. All 206 E2E checks pass with the verified binary (4 CLI plus 202 config/runtime checks). Logs are recorded in `tmp/request-host-validation/`.

### Command/provider capability hosts — 2026-10-14

`src/command_host.v` defines required Codex, Feishu, and generic command capabilities. The three handlers no longer store nil App pointers or reference the concrete App type. Feishu buffer state is injected through a typed state accessor; application adapters in `src/app_command_host.v` delegate bridge proxy operations. Existing command routing, target resolution, statuses, and error propagation are preserved.

Feishu bridge client and server callbacks recover heap-allocated, required callback contexts from the WebSocket API's `voidptr`, rather than recovering App. These contexts carry a narrow dispatch/proxy host and a required bridge state reference. Client reconnect/heartbeat and server-session internals use those capabilities; request normalization remains in the App adapter. Provider WebSocket closures capture `ProviderWebSocketHost`; Feishu heartbeat uses FeishuState directly, removing the unsafe App aliases from post-connect closures. OpenAI frame mapping uses an optional single-method mapper host with an explicit missing-host result, and the HTTP progress callback now directly recovers its dedicated stream state instead of manipulating pointer storage. Four unsafe App conversion sites and six nil App/state reference defaults are removed in this slice.

Validation: strict V3 build with DB enabled, 7 command/provider/Feishu/OpenAI test files, and all 6 codexbot-fast files (52 test functions) pass on macOS. New narrow-host regressions cover live command mutation, admin restart results, bridge pending-result delivery, callback/proxy dispatch, error/close cleanup, and optional frame mapper behavior without constructing App. The initial gateway run found an existing SSE assertion that assumed `id` was the first JSON key; it now parses each SSE chunk and checks its fields, and the complete gateway suite passes on rerun. All 206 E2E checks pass with the verified binary. Safety lint now scans all of `src` for unsafe App conversions and guards the concrete-App-free handler/client boundaries; `git diff --check` passes. Logs are recorded in `tmp/command-provider-validation/`.

### Required command/provider context injection — 2026-10-14

All 19 nil callback defaults in `command.RuntimeContext`, `provider.RuntimeContext`, `provider.RuntimeDispatchContext`, and `ProviderInstanceRuntimeContext` are replaced with required constructor fields. These callbacks are invoked unconditionally; optional handler/static-spec lookup continues to be expressed by the callback result. CommandExecutor, ProviderSpec, and ProviderRuntimeAdapter also require their context fields, preventing an aggregate constructor from silently creating an incomplete context. Existing App builders already inject all production callbacks. Tests with intentional empty behavior explicitly choose `provider.RuntimeContext.noop()`, implemented with named safe functions. The shutdown fixture now registers its provider with the real App-built context instead of constructing an empty context and assigning it later.

Validation: strict V3 build with DB enabled, 8 related command/provider/instance/registry/lifecycle/OpenAI test files, safety lint, and `git diff --check` pass on macOS. New tests exercise callback arguments, mutations of original captured state, error propagation, explicit no-op lifecycle behavior, dispatch URL failures, instance normalization, and existing apply-error handling. Three compiler-negative probes reject incomplete command, provider lifecycle, and provider dispatch contexts. Safety lint rejects both nil defaults and callback fields lacking required injection.

Parallel compilation became unusually slow while other project builds were active; the remaining integration checks completed in isolated/serial runs. The new contract also exposed the shutdown fixture's deferred context assignment, which was corrected before its successful rerun. Two E2E attempts missed the provider handshake probe deadline. The final build and the preceding slice's passing binary have identical SHA-256 hashes; after compilation ended and this slice's leftover test processes were cleaned up, an isolated rerun passed all 206 E2E checks with the original timeout unchanged. Logs and expected compiler failures are recorded in `tmp/runtime-context-injection-validation/`.

### Provider lifecycle context ownership — 2026-10-14

ProviderSpec is now the sole registered lifecycle-context holder. ProviderRuntimeAdapter retains only its required Provider and forwards the caller's context to start, stop, and snapshot. All five bootstrap/registry adapter construction sites drop their extra context field. ProviderSpec provides the lifecycle invocation methods used by admin and generic runtime snapshots and shutdown. Shutdown captures a list of complete specs under App.mu and invokes stop outside the lock; the two parallel runtime/context arrays and their hub wrapper are removed, preserving the prior map iteration behavior and stop-error reporting.

Validation: strict V3 build with DB enabled, 4 related lifecycle/bootstrap/registry/server test files, safety lint, and `git diff --check` pass on macOS. New regressions replace a registered spec's context and verify that start, shutdown, admin snapshots, and generic runtime snapshots use the replacement; they also verify that context mutations made by a Provider pass back through the adapter to the caller. Safety lint prevents RuntimeContext storage from returning to ProviderRuntimeAdapter. All 206 E2E checks pass on the first run with the verified binary. Logs are recorded in `tmp/provider-lifecycle-context-validation/`.

Linux/production, full-suite, and codexbot lifecycle coverage remain pending. The VM preflight still found pre-existing unrelated tracked `.qoder` changes; Linux validation stopped under the Linux testing skill's recorded preflight rule. Next: continue the remaining runtime ownership and safety work before closing the milestones.

### Provider bootstrap registration and context construction — 2026-10-14

Each enabled provider now assembles one complete ProviderSpec with one lifecycle context, then registers and runs that spec. Bootstrap no longer calls the generic registration API that constructed a second context. Full-spec registration synchronizes both provider lookup maps. The complete spec is visible before init; init mutations are published before start, and final context mutations are retained on successful startup and init/start failure. Existing failure events remain unchanged.

Validation: final DB-enabled V3 build, 4 related bootstrap/lifecycle/registry/server test files, safety lint, and git diff --check pass. New regressions cover complete-spec visibility, lifecycle context mutation and failure persistence, error events, and shutdown. Original isolated provider and SSE scenarios pass on both the previous verified binary and the new binary. Logs are recorded in tmp/provider-bootstrap-validation/.

Initial full E2E attempts stopped at provider WebSocket handshake waiting, SSE HTTP 404, and handshake waiting respectively; the second attempt passed the provider scenarios. The follow-up below resolves this validation gap. Linux validation stopped at the existing unrelated tracked .qoder changes under the recorded VM preflight rule. Full-suite and codexbot lifecycle coverage remain pending.

### Provider WebSocket and SSE E2E stability — 2026-10-14

The provider upstream probe now consumes WebSocket bytes in Node's HTTP upgrade head buffer. A deterministic integration regression sends the upgrade request and masked frames in one write: the previous probe times out, while the fixed probe completes. The unchanged 10-second protocol deadline now begins at upgrade instead of counting vhttpd cold startup. Each independent acceptance scenario stops and reaps its test processes; relay startup no longer registers PIDs in a discarded command-substitution subshell. This removes accumulated servers/workers/embedded runtimes from later scenarios. Preserved timing logs show the original probe expiring around late startup, before the handshake dispatch completed.

The SSE demo now supplies a generator factory instead of sharing a consumed Generator across connections. Repeated stream opens previously reproduced a Cannot rewind a generator that was already run exception. PHP regression coverage verifies completion/reopen, interruption/reconnect, and independent readers. Acceptance coverage now checks a second complete SSE connection. Optional diagnostic artifact retention is available without changing normal cleanup.

Validation: complete E2E passes with 4 CLI and 203 config/runtime checks, FAIL 0, plus the Node and PHP regressions. Provider handshake/message/ack and SSE first-open/reopen pass. Safety lint, diff and script syntax checks pass. Evidence is recorded in tmp/e2e-stability-validation/. This slice changes the fixtures, example and test lifecycle; it uses the already verified V3 binary. Linux remains pending under the recorded VM preflight rule.

### StreamRuntimeContext explicit injection — 2026-10-14

StreamRuntimeContext removes all four nil callback defaults. Emit/open/next/close callbacks and a narrow open-failure renderer are required constructor inputs. app_stream_runtime.v owns App assembly and the existing dispatch/direct-stream entry adapters. Stream dispatch no longer receives App; the open-error response uses the injected renderer. The context, dispatch execution, and direct stream execution files contain no App reference. Existing fallback, HTTP 500 error metadata, events, and close behavior are preserved.

Validation: final DB-enabled V3 build, 4 related context/stream-projection/server test files, and safety lint pass. Three new regressions verify callback/correlation forwarding, App-free fallback/error rendering, and best-effort close state/error injection. A negative compiler probe using the actual context source rejects an empty constructor with five required-field errors. ### Managed worker process registry ownership — 2026-10-14

The process_runtime module owns one process-wide ManagedProcessRegistry of actual os.Process handles, with serialized launch, observation, retirement and shutdown. Initial start, ensure/retry and restart all register immediately through the same entry point. Normal stop, failed readiness and natural exit remove the owner record before reap; a stale handle cannot unregister a replacement merely because its numeric PID matches. WNOWAIT observes exit without freeing the PID before group cleanup. Snapshots prune exited children, and process shutdown drains the registry and rejects later launches. Transport/worker paths no longer maintain a raw global PID array or independently spawn/probe/reap managed children. Admin snapshots suppress retired PID/RSS fields.

SIGINT/SIGTERM callbacks only perform an errno-preserving nonblocking pipe write; an ordinary thread logs and drains the registry. The pipe is close-on-exec. Active-runtime registration/config snapshots are synchronized, and App summary callbacks run after releasing that registry lock. Normal/error return from the server entry also drains the process owner.

The blocked pipe test was sampled and stopped, and the known orphan from the failed restart fixture was identified by its unique path/PGID and reaped. Corrected regressions pass and no test processes remain.

### Executor concurrent shutdown ownership — 2026-10-14

Executor close is terminal and has one synchronized admission boundary. Lane acquisition, all five bounded queue submissions, mailbox scheduling and bootstrap reject new work after stopping begins. Queue submissions never block while holding the lifecycle mutex. Pending mailbox entries complete with an explicit closed error; a scheduler that selected a task before stopping cancels its task instead of restoring it. The owner notifies every lane worker before joining any of them; workers drain accepted tasks through the same loop and block normally when idle. Signature refresh is joined, and direct lane leases, mailbox schedulers and host builders finish before module/session destruction. Concurrent close callers await the same completed teardown.

Wakeup scheduling is disabled and sleeping deliveries notice cancellation promptly. Host remembrance and late actor/affinity binding writes cannot restore closed state. Actor probes now transfer one lane lease to the worker instead of releasing it twice; failed direct WebSocket and affinity submissions release the caller-owned lease.

### Cache and DB listener generation ownership — 2026-10-15

Cache and DB listeners now share listener_runtime.Owner. It holds one optional listener and serializes startup, publication, stop and deferred cleanup by generation. Production startup reserves synchronously before spawning, so a delayed serving loop cannot undo a subsequent stop. Duplicate starts are rejected before touching the socket path; DB provider start reports db_listener_busy. A candidate that loses publication remains local to its serving loop and is closed there. Published handles are closed/unlinked exactly once by their generation's finalizer; stale finalizers and state/error updates cannot affect a replacement.

Stop returns an optional generation instead of transferring a listener to another thread. Provider stop and cache shutdown wait for that generation outside App.mu. Waiting ignores newer generations. DB keeps the reservation through session/pool cleanup, preventing a new listener from starting while old shared-resource cleanup runs. The listening socket lifetime is separate from the existing accepted-request lifecycle.

The shared listen helper explicitly sets the Unix listener nonblocking and uses a 100 ms accept timeout. Real socket regression tests exposed that the current V library otherwise blocks in its initial accept call despite an accept timeout. Idle polling does not record runtime errors, and accepted sockets observed after stop are closed instead of dispatched.

### Upstream and bridge reconnect/heartbeat ownership — 2026-10-15

WebSocketRuntime now owns a connection_runtime.Registry instead of a permanent started-key map. Each reconnect run reserves before spawning and releases by identity. Provider stop pauses admission until explicit start; application shutdown closes admission permanently. Provider and process shutdown notify all upstream and Feishu background owners before waiting, outside App locks and before disposing engines/plugins.

Each connected attempt has a Session with broadcast stop state and an owned duplicate socket descriptor. Stop wakes the reader through shutdown on that descriptor, preventing a late stop from touching a recycled original descriptor. The WebSocket reader retains closing responsibility. Session teardown stops and joins the task handles returned by post-connect before releasing borrowed connection references and its interrupt descriptor. Backoff, heartbeat and initialization delays are interruptible; a stopped endpoint lookup cannot publish a late connection. Failed HTTP upgrades close unpublished TCP connections. Codex initialization write failures stop the session and follow the reconnect cleanup path.

Outbound card bridge uses the same upstream loop. Feishu heartbeat, Codex heartbeat/initialization, bridge heartbeat and Feishu buffer flusher now have explicit owners and completion waits. Reconnect joins the old session tasks before creating another attempt; final disconnect state is applied after those tasks exit. In-flight endpoint requests, dial/handshake operations and business callbacks still finish cooperatively under their existing network/execution deadlines; this change does not force-free executing native resources.

### Accepted cache/DB request ownership — 2026-10-15

Cache and DB listeners now register every accepted connection with their listener generation before spawning its handler. Dispatch admission is synchronized with stop. The owner retains only unfinished requests, removes records on completion, and refuses to finish the generation while handlers remain. A stopped generation cannot publish late work into a restarted listener.

Each request owns a duplicate interrupt descriptor created atomically with F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC. Stop shuts down this owned descriptor to wake blocked frame reads/writes without closing a borrowed, potentially recycled descriptor. The handler closes its original connection before completing its token; completion releases the duplicate exactly once. The close-on-exec assertion prevents the new interrupt descriptor from surviving worker exec.

The serving loop drains handlers outside application locks before listener close/unlink. DB teardown waits for admitted dispatch to return before cleaning transaction sessions and closing the pool, and retains its generation reservation throughout cleanup. Native DB calls remain cooperative: shutdown does not force-free resources underneath executing driver code.

### Transaction session leases and retirement — 2026-10-15

The DB transaction registry now retains TransactionSession owners rather than copies of native SessionHandle values. Each entry transfers its optional handle to one ConnectionLease at a time. Other borrowers wait outside App.mu; native driver methods also serialize against release/retirement on the lease itself. Regular release returns the mutated handle to its transaction owner. Released leases reject driver access, and repeated/stale release cannot end a newer lease.

Commit and rollback acquire an exclusive terminal lease and then close admission for the entry. Retirement keeps that entry active and registered until reset, pool return or discard finishes. Detachment checks owner identity. Ordinary SQL errors release the lease while retaining the transaction; connection loss and terminal failures retire it once. All acquisition paths have a fallback deferred release, including nontransactional ping errors. Missing transaction lookup returns invalid_session without creating a pool.

Runtime.request_stop closes transaction admission and wakes parked borrowers before listener request draining begins. Cleanup waits outside App locks for executing leases, attempts rollback on abandoned transactions, then closes their handles before pool teardown. It no longer enables MySQL autocommit before abandoning a transaction. Failed PostgreSQL handles use the native pool's release/replacement lifecycle rather than opening a temporary replacement pool.

### DB pool construction, refill and shutdown ownership — 2026-10-15

DB runtime now owns one PoolOwner with an explicit optional pool, replacing the copied native handle and independent readiness flag. Physical creation and close run outside App.mu; concurrent closers wait for one teardown, with generation checks protecting restart.

ConnectionLease and transaction sessions retain their originating pool, generation and one-time capacity permit. Capacity admission includes idle transactions and wakes parked borrowers on stop before listener draining. Running driver calls remain cooperative, and borrower ordering has no FIFO guarantee.

### MCP SSE stream ownership and shutdown — 2026-10-15

The remaining resource review found that MCP stream workers resolved their connection by session ID on every flush. A replaced worker could therefore drain the replacement stream's queue, while expiry and eviction removed session records without retiring the associated stream task. HTTP header-write failure after connection takeover also lacked an owning cleanup path.

SessionStream now owns each transferred TCP connection and a close-on-exec interrupt descriptor. Session records retain an explicit optional stream reference, and McpState separately tracks accepted streams until physical cleanup completes. Registration precedes header writing and spawn; the HTTP adapter retains cleanup responsibility until handoff succeeds. Headers, complete SSE message frames, keepalive and close share one per-stream write lock. Cancellation interrupts socket I/O through the owned duplicate; only the stream cleanup path closes the original connection.

Replacement, deletion, TTL expiry and capacity eviction cancel the affected stream by identity. A retiring worker flushes only its own still-current binding, and identity-checked cleanup cannot detach a replacement or a recreated session ID. Process shutdown closes stream admission, notifies MCP together with the upstream/bridge owners, then waits outside application/protocol locks before engine teardown. Client-capability lookup now holds the session mutex. Safety lint guards against restoring session-ID-based binding and flushing in the HTTP stream adapter.

### Executor lane wakeup ownership and shutdown — 2026-10-15

Each host/session now owns a callback scope with a required state reference. Each scheduled wakeup has a separate heap identity, registered and counted before spawn. Deadline lookup uses explicit optional results instead of an empty wakeup placeholder. Replacement, cancellation, reset and failed bootstrap retire only the matching scope/job; repeated completion cannot decrement the count twice. Host destruction retires callbacks before releasing module/session handles, and executor close rejects new wakeups, cancels sleepers, drains workers and waits for all registered wakeup tasks before destroying hosts.

### WebSocket interrupt descriptor ownership across exec — 2026-10-15

The remaining handle review found that connection_runtime.Session created its owned interrupt socket with plain dup. That clears close-on-exec protection, allowing an executed worker child to inherit the auxiliary descriptor. Session.connected now uses atomic F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC, matching the policy already used by listener request and MCP stream interruption handles. The existing native duplication error is preserved.

The constructor explicitly rejects a WebSocket client whose nullable library TCP field is not connected. Session retains the optional interrupt descriptor; release closes only this owned duplicate, leaving the borrowed connection to the client lifecycle. A failed duplicate does not close other connections, and late stop/release keeps its existing protection against recycled descriptors. Safety lint rejects plain dup in this production owner.

### Shutdown signal pipe installation ownership — 2026-10-15

Shutdown signal registration now has one process-lifetime owner with an explicit optional pipe and serialized installation. Repeated or concurrent installation is rejected after the first success, preserving the original callback, pipe and waiter. Candidate descriptors are closed on every failed installation; the native signal callback sees a volatile sig_atomic_t descriptor published only after both handlers are installed. Successful pipes remain alive until process exit so in-flight signal callbacks cannot access a retired descriptor.

SIGTERM installation failure restores the former SIGINT disposition. A restoration failure is reported explicitly, leaves the candidate unpublished and allows a later installation retry; a remaining callback has no published fd to write. Signals handled during the uncommitted installation window are not queued. This change does not add dynamic handler replacement or shutdown-time pipe retirement.

### Owned inbound WebSocket sessions and close tasks — 2026-10-15

Inbound worker, dispatch, relay and Feishu bridge WebSocket sessions now share a terminal IngressOwner retained by the App's WebSocket hub. Each scope reserves before spawn and retains CLOEXEC interrupt descriptors for its accepted TCP socket and, for worker bridges, its connected Unix socket. Worker open explicitly injects a required connection-registration callback before handshake I/O; cancellation/rejection closes the socket and returns the worker reservation. HTTP shutdown notifies every App's inbound owner before waiting, joins all accepted sessions and close tasks, then continues with existing background/auxiliary and executor/plugin/provider teardown. Late reservations and attachments are rejected.

The serving scope owns original socket close, hub/bridge cleanup and worker release; stop only interrupts retained descriptors. Worker message/close callbacks no longer close/unregister the same worker socket independently. Dispatch opening-state failure cleanup is explicit, and relay finalization also detaches its owned carrier. A managed SDK adapter captures the native client in its single accept callback and publishes application registrations after the upgrade response succeeds; EOF and failed upgrade writes converge on native-client cleanup.

Hub asynchronous close tasks now derive a retained socket from the actual serving run, rather than duplicating a possibly recycled native client fd. They serialize close-frame writes, wake the reader and finish their reservation; the reader retains sole responsibility for native Client.close. Background child runs cannot be mistaken for serving sessions. Logical closing and the one-time socket-close claim are separate, so an already closing dispatch session can still send its close frame. Requested close code/reason remain available to close callbacks.

### Provider WebSocket hello deadline: confirmed cause and fix — 2026-10-15

The provider handshake investigation confirms that the old upstream path upgraded the WebSocket before lazily creating the plugin runtime, installing the Node/TypeScript profile, loading the module and running its handshake hook. Server executor warmup used a separate owner and did not warm this plugin. This cold work consumed the probe's existing 10-second post-upgrade hello/push/ack budget; the original intermittent failure dispatched about 10.3 seconds after connection.

Each connection attempt now prepares and parses its handshake result before opening the transport. A required prepare_fn returns that attempt's activation callback; activation sends the prepared result without invoking the JS handshake hook again. Stop/enabled checks reject late connections after preparation, preparation errors fail before transport creation, and activation errors follow owned session cleanup without starting heartbeat jobs. Every reconnect prepares a fresh result. Bridge and no-hook factories inject explicit no-op preparation. A handshake_prepared event records the cost separately from actual handshake_dispatched.

Debug evidence also found fixture feedback: probe output JSON was inside the default signature root. The probe writes it on successful completion or deadline expiry, so it could trigger another load after failure/completion. It was not the initial timeout trigger. The fixture now tracks only its generated handler; global JSON source watching remains unchanged. A new real E2E assertion verifies prepared -> connected -> dispatched ordering.

The controlled old/new comparison uses the same 11-second handshake preparation and unchanged 10-second peer deadline. The old binary fails with no hello/ack even after excluding probe JSON. The fixed binary spends 16,462 ms preparing before connection and then passes all nine hello/push/ack/pipeline/order checks. Three normal cold launches also pass nine checks each, with preparation costs of 4,626 / 6,299 / 4,662 ms and no host resets.

All 32 distinct V functions across five related files pass, including five new preparation/cancellation/failure/reconnect lifecycle regressions. The actual JS handshake/normalize fixture retains its 3-second exchange assertions after preparation. ### MCP and dispatch WebSocket session generations — 2026-10-16

Dispatch WebSocket open, message and close now reuse one runtime context and executor port. Command dispatch uses the retained provider and checks session identity after the RPC.

TTL expiry, eviction and deletion release bindings with their session; process shutdown clears remaining routes and uses the existing stream owner to stop/join native connections. Retirement probes only read generation liveness and must never re-enter the MCP owner. New-generation sessions remain registered.

Provider dispatch contexts previously copied ProviderRuntimeHub (including mutex-bearing runtime state) into closures while resolving launch URLs through the current App. Directory construction now projects instance names, readiness/bootstrap/enabled flags and Codex launch URLs/errors into ordinary data. The App publication lock protects directory assembly; the Codex state lock covers its configuration reads. Returned instance arrays are cloned. Read-only construction and launch projection do not initialize Codex runtime instances; the explicit ensure operation still registers instances with the same configuration resolver.

Admin context construction now captures one worker metrics value, the selected executor and pipeline/provider directory under the publication lock. Executor description callbacks run after unlocking, allowing callback re-entry without deadlock. Subsequent reads from that context retain the captured configuration and engine view; a fresh context observes a new publication. Existing live counters continue to read their actual subsystem owners. Snapshot values do not own connections, workers, sessions or shutdown.

Validation passed: 68 distinct related test functions (7 new, 61 existing) across provider registry/bootstrap/lifecycle, instance context, admin state/apps/graph, Codex runtime and context ownership. The related suite runs with DB disabled because two registry checks explicitly require the uncompiled DB contract. Separately, the V3 production build with DB enabled passed, as did formatting, safety lint and diff checks. Full E2E passed (204 config/runtime plus 4 CLI checks), together with the Node upgrade probe, both managed-worker signal lifecycle tests and PHP stream reopen/reconnect/readers. Exact input hashes, isolated diff, logs and retained fixtures are under `tmp/provider-admin-snapshot-validation/`.

Feishu endpoint discovery remains an explicit live network operation, and provider lifecycle contexts retain their existing start/stop owners. The admin response is not a global atomic sample of every live subsystem counter. ## Provider instance control commit ownership — 2026-10-16

Unified command and admin instance updates behind a validated commit. Configuration decoding happens before mutation; under App.mu the operation patches the current domain runtime, publishes the desired-state registry row and captures its admin response. Network startup runs after unlocking and resolves current configuration, so delayed startup cannot replay an obsolete prepared patch. Ensure resolves/reapplies the current stored spec under the same boundary. Invalid configuration leaves both registry and runtime unchanged; concurrent admin responses retain the metadata from their own commit.

ProviderInstanceRegistry owns a shared map mutex used by upsert/get/list. Production admin iteration now uses those reads rather than traversing the raw map. Instance admin, provider catalog and built-in runtime snapshot reads coordinate with the App commit boundary; lifecycle snapshot callbacks use the same read path. The lock order is App publication, domain runtime, then short registry reads where needed; registry operations release their lock before domain callbacks or network work.

Codex control updates patch only explicitly supplied JSON fields, fixing the model-only update that previously injected a default URL. They preserve connection/activity/session fields from the current runtime. A configuration generation prevents delayed Codex activity updates from restoring older command configuration while allowing their activity fields to publish. Configure/apply/ensure participate in App shutdown admission, so accepted operations are joined before cleanup and new control writes are rejected after shutdown begins; snapshot reads remain available.

Validation passed: 117 distinct related test functions (10 new, 107 existing). New regressions cover invalid-command rollback, failed admin updates, concurrent command commits and per-admin-response ownership, concurrent snapshot readers, partial patches preserving runtime state, stale activity publication, ensure/upsert interleaving, concurrent registry access and shutdown rejection. The suite uses the registry tests' uncompiled-DB configuration; the V3 production build separately passed with DB enabled. Formatting, safety lint and diff checks passed. Full E2E passed: 204 config/runtime and 4 CLI checks, plus the Node upgrade probe, both managed-worker signal lifecycle tests and PHP stream checks. Exact inputs, isolated diff, source hashes and retained fixtures are in `tmp/provider-instance-control-validation/`.

Legacy low-level registry-only and injected context helpers retain their contracts; built-in admin/command operations use the unified commit. This slice does not make all unrelated live runtime activity updates globally atomic, nor does it change reconnect/restart policy. ### Replacement control publication snapshots — 2026-10-16

Replacement preview, preparation and application now capture one plan generation together with listener, scheme and build inputs under App.mu. Preview reads reserve a sequence before file I/O: a delayed older read or a preview of a displaced plan cannot overwrite a newer preview record. Lightweight and prepared application validate the captured generation at publication; a conflict returns runtime_plan_replacement_stale_base and retires only the unpublished candidate outside the lock. Public replacement workflows retain their existing admission and shutdown scopes.

Successful publication returns a receipt captured inside the commit lock. Replacement events use that receipt and the operation's preview/pending values, including the existing lightweight reload_transforms field, rather than re-reading App after retirement callbacks. Pending state and retained/returned history clone collection buffers, including nested action targets. A refresh error is associated only with its captured pending generation. Published RuntimePlan values remain immutable and the views own no native resource handles.

### MCP route lookup and metadata callback ownership — 2026-10-16

The remaining ownership audit found that MCP route assembly called executor.provider() while holding App.mu, allowing a reentrant metadata callback to deadlock publication. It also constructed a current-engine candidate before resolving an already-bound session. The protocol owner now exposes an explicit optional route lookup: missing, expired, retired and stopped sessions reject admission; an existing bound route returns directly without invoking unrelated current-engine metadata. For new/unbound routes, App.mu captures only state and the executor port; metadata runs outside that lock, and the candidate retains the captured executor/provider pair. Final protocol admission rechecks shutdown and candidate retirement before binding, preserving a concurrently selected existing route.

Nine new regressions cover reentrant metadata publication, a blocked callback permitting concurrent publication, retained routes skipping current metadata, shutdown/retirement/deletion during candidate construction, optional lookup states and expiry. ### HEAD stream completion and resource release — 2026-10-16

The remaining resource audit found that streaming HEAD requests suppressed payload writes but still consumed dispatch-next responses or direct worker frames until stream completion. An endless SSE could therefore retain a HEAD request and its resources indefinitely. Non-SSE HEAD responses also wrote a terminating chunk after their headers. Dispatch HEAD now skips next calls and body framing, then closes the logical stream once with its captured open state. Direct SSE/passthrough HEAD skips worker reads and body framing, returning to the existing outer owner for worker-socket close and request-lease completion. GET behavior and existing ownership boundaries are preserved.

Three new regression functions cover SSE/text, open done/not-done, best-effort close failure, case-normalized HEAD, unread direct worker frames, HTTP descriptor release and physical worker close before exactly-once lease completion. The existing GET/HEAD regression now checks headers-only HEAD and the open state used for close. All 38 related functions across seven files pass; the final DB-enabled V3 build, formatting verification, safety lint and git diff --check pass. Complete E2E passes all 208 CLI/config/runtime checks plus Node and PHP regressions. The tested binary is installed as vhttpd. Exact sources, hashes, isolated diff, logs and retained E2E fixtures are in tmp/stream-head-lifecycle-validation/.

HEAD still uses the existing open/close callbacks and their transport timeouts; this slice removes body consumption and does not introduce a new cancellation protocol for external worker processes. Linux remains stopped by the recorded skill preflight rule because the mounted checkout has unrelated tracked changes. Full V-suite, load/live-DB and wider subsystem audits remain separate follow-ups.

### Bridge queued-send connection selection — 2026-10-16

The remaining connection audit found that both bridge send directions borrowed a registry client before waiting for card_bridge_send_mu. The inbound send path also drops an unnecessary unsafe pointer alias. Existing identity-checked removal and native close ownership are preserved.

### Bridge in-flight send leases and native close ownership — 2026-10-17

Bridge registries now hold one connection_runtime.WebSocketSender per published connection rather than a raw Client. Registration returns that exact owner to the client/server callback state; queued sends, heartbeat and callback replies share its admission and lease accounting. Replacement/withdrawal retires the old owner without waiting under the registry lock. Callback state retains its own generation and cannot send through a replacement; teardown clears optional owners and joins accepted work outside registry locks. The reader/session remains the sole native close owner.

Each send holds a read lease on the original ClientState while native Client.close must acquire its write lock to enter closing before TCP/TLS teardown. A writer snapshot captured on the registration/reader thread preserves the native frame-write lock and transport references without copying a concurrently running reader. It has an independent open state, avoiding recursive original-state lock acquisition while close is waiting. Under V3, a shared-field struct update preserves the original shared reference, so the constructor uses a narrowly scoped compiler-derived pointer-slot copy to detach only that reference. State-isolation assertions and actual TCP/WSS close tests cover this compatibility boundary. Native connection identity checks reject a reused Client generation; the owner never closes borrowed native handles.

Validation: all 54 distinct related functions pass (31 bridge/application and 23 connection-runtime), including 9 new functions. They cover owner replacement/withdrawal, callback/registry owner identity, close joining successful and failed sends, terminal admission, actual WSS frames, interruption of a blocked infinite write before close, native Client replacement and recycled-fd protection. The final DB-enabled V3 build, format verification, safety lint, Python fixture syntax and git diff --check pass. Complete E2E passes all 208 CLI/config/runtime checks plus Node and PHP regressions. ### Unix listener pathname ownership — 2026-10-17

The shared listener_runtime handle now owns both the native descriptor and the socket pathname identity for cache, DB and internal admin. Startup binds without the SDK's implicit unlink; cache/DB no longer remove paths before binding. Existing live sockets, regular files, dangling symlinks and stale sockets are rejected without replacement. Stale sockets now require explicit cleanup after confirming their former owner has stopped. Failed construction closes its descriptor; successful descriptors are close-on-exec and retain nonblocking accept polling.

After request drain, the serving owner closes this handle once and only unlinks a socket entry whose device/inode still matches its captured identity. Withdrawn candidates use the same cleanup. Independent owners competing for a path have one kernel bind winner. Old finalization preserves a replacement already present at its identity check. This is lifecycle coordination for cooperating listeners, not atomic protection against arbitrary external filesystem changes between lstat and unlink.

Validation: 69 distinct related functions pass (13 listener-runtime, 8 cache, 27 DB and 21 application/resource tests), including 9 new regression functions. They cover competing independent starts, existing files/symlinks/live and stale sockets, replacement socket/file preservation, repeat close, conflict reservation release and DB shared-pool preservation. A cache fixture now waits for synchronized started publication rather than pathname existence; bind creates the entry before listen is ready. An isolated SDK-startup comparison fails 4 of the 6 new path regressions as expected. Final DB-enabled V3 production build, helper/test format verification, safety lint and git diff --check pass. Complete E2E passes 208 CLI/config/runtime checks plus Node upgrade/PID and PHP stream regressions. The tested binary is installed as vhttpd. ### Process-owned PID files — 2026-10-17

PID files now belong to process_runtime.PidFiles rather than App shutdown bindings or configuration filenames. Configuration resolution prepares parent directories without publishing a PID. Single-server startup acquires its file only after bind preflight; multi-server startup preflights every listener before acquiring all distinct paths as a batch. Native exclusive creation preserves existing files and symlinks, including stale PID entries; stale files require explicit cleanup after confirming the former owner has stopped. Startup acquisition failures are logged for both entry paths.

The owner retains close-on-exec descriptors and fstat identities, deduplicates shared listener paths, verifies repeated acquisition against identity/content, and rolls back only newly created files when a batch fails. Terminal, idempotent cleanup checks device/inode and PID text before unlinking, then closes each owned descriptor once. Ordinary server exit cleans up after App finalization; signal exit cleans up after App and managed-process shutdown. Configuration-derived PID deletions were removed from App finalization and signal handling. Replacement inodes and in-place content changes survive cleanup. External filesystem mutation between identity/content checks and unlink remains outside this cooperating-owner synchronization boundary.

Validation: 118 distinct related V functions pass (24 process-runtime and 94 configuration/startup/finalization/signal/application functions), including 12 new functions. The new real-process E2E file adds 5 passing cases for SIGTERM/SIGINT, competing processes, failed bind preflight and same-PID replacement inode preservation. The retained previous binary fails 3 of those 5 cases as expected. An initial integrated run exposed a missing startup diagnostic; logging and an entry-level regression were added. The provider E2E fixture also gave two independent services one PID path; those services now have distinct paths, preserving the production exclusivity rule.

### Shared multi-listener App construction and startup ownership — 2026-10-17

A pure runtime-plan query now selects each listener's available App owner before allocation. Multi-listener configuration validates targets first, resolves actual owners before forward aliases, and injects the owner's concrete executor plan into aliases without constructing another executor. Alias endpoint/TLS/listener metadata stays distinct. The independent admin-plane configuration also follows the actual App owner when its selected listener is an alias.

App construction allocates only real owners, then assembles listener bindings in their original order. The former construct-then-overwrite sharing step was removed: VJSX constructors already start lane workers, so replacing those Apps previously abandoned executor/plugin workers. Startup and shutdown now use the same identity-based canonical binding selection. Each shared App initializes and warms up once using the configuration that constructed it, while every listener still serves its own endpoint.

Resolved executors have an explicit handoff boundary. Failed configuration assembly closes newly resolved owner executors; preflight/PID acquisition failures close untransferred executors once per real owner. The single-mode multi-server fallback consumes its already resolved configuration instead of resolving and allocating again. Successfully built Apps retain normal finalization ownership.

### Ownership follow-ups reconciled after documentation service recovery — 2026-10-17

The following four checkpoints were validated while OpenKnowledge was unavailable. Their code and logs are retained locally; documentation is now reconciled without changing the earlier checkpoint history.

#### Admin state file-store directory and I/O ownership

Independent request-local FileStore values previously had independent mutexes. [File-store operations](../src/admin_state_store/store.v) now share a stable directory lock inode across requests, Apps and processes; each operation borrows and closes its own close-on-exec descriptor. Readers share a lease and writers use an exclusive lease. The lock file stays in place so waiters retain the same synchronization identity. [Native file ownership](../src/admin_state_store/files.c.v) keeps UUID/O_EXCL temporary files until exact-path rename commits, cleans ordinary failures and rolls back a failed event append's partial tail. Three before-source probes reproduce the original read/append/rename defects. Synchronization is per cooperating operation, without a transaction across draft/metadata/event calls or a new fsync/crash-durability guarantee.

#### Relay MCP session completion and dispatch ownership

Relay initialization previously registered an unbound session, allowing a later RPC to bind to a replacement executor. [Shared completion](../src/mcp_session_completion.v) now takes the protocol owner and captured dispatch route for HTTP and relay. [Relay dispatch](../src/relay_pipeline_runtime.v) preserves existing request session identity, retains initialization capabilities and queues response messages against that route and the existing sampling policy. Conflicting initialization returns 409. Deleted/stopped/retired owners cannot receive late publication, and runtime retirement can find relay-created sessions and stop/wait their SSE streams through the existing native-close owner. A retained original-method probe confirms missing route/capabilities/messages and incorrect generation/response-ID behavior.

#### Upload persistence and ordinary-failure cleanup

HTTP uploads previously wrote directly to their final path, leaving partial files on write failure. [Upload storage](../src/upload_store/file.c.v) owns an exclusive UUID temporary file and close-on-exec descriptor until all bytes are written and the descriptor is closed. Exact-path, non-overwriting publication transfers the completed file to persistent storage; ordinary failures reclaim the unpublished path. Existing files, directories and symlinks survive collisions, including a target appearing during the write. [HTTP upload regressions](../src/upload_persistence_owner_test.v) use a separate RLIMIT_FSIZE process: original os.write_file leaves 4096 bytes, while the corrected HTTP route returns 500 with no upload/temp file or upload.completed event. The shared directory remains operator-owned. Publication requires same-directory filesystem hard-link support; no copy/overwrite fallback, fsync guarantee or forced-process-death temporary-file sweeping is added.

#### MCP admin snapshot publication ownership

[MCP snapshots](../src/api/mcp/protocol/state.v) previously captured sessions under the owner mutex but read limits, TTL, origin arrays and sampling policy after unlocking. Hot reload could mix complete settings publications and race origin-array copying. A private snapshot_view now captures pruning decisions, filtered rows and normalized configuration under one lock. A pure page helper sorts and paginates detached rows after releasing it; no live session, stream, dispatch token or settings array is retained. Negative bounds are clamped and page-end calculation uses remaining rows to avoid overflow. [Snapshot tests](../src/api/mcp/protocol/snapshot_test.v) cover retained views, concurrent publications, native cleanup ownership after expiry, defaults/filters and extreme pagination. An isolated before-source probe inserts a test-only scheduling point after the first original limit read and demonstrates old capacity mixed with new TTL/origins/policy in both brief and detailed responses.

| Checkpoint | Related permanent V functions passed | New functions | Retained evidence |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Admin file store | 45 | 7 | `tmp/admin-store-owner-validation/` |
| Relay MCP ownership | 75 | 7 | `tmp/relay-mcp-owner-validation/` |
| Upload persistence | 66 | 8 | `tmp/upload-owner-validation/` |
| MCP snapshot ownership | 51 | 5 | `tmp/mcp-snapshot-owner-validation/` |

These are per-checkpoint counts with overlapping suites, not an aggregate unique-test count. Relay and snapshot evidence also retain separate before-source probes. The current tested binary includes all four changes; its final build has 434 staged production V inputs. ### Transform execution target and discarded result ownership — 2026-10-17

[TransformDispatchView](../src/transform_dispatch_view.v) captures transform definitions and the engine registry together under App.mu. [Pipeline views](../src/pipeline_dispatch_view.v) now retain borrowed engine references; HTTP, event, relay and upload completion pass the retained transform view through the chain. [VJSX event dispatch](../src/vjsx_event_runtime.v) can use that exact registry instead of re-reading App for each transform. Named lookup and existing fallback semantics operate inside the captured registry; retired origins return their own admission error rather than adopting a live replacement.

[VJSX transforms](../src/vjsx_transformer_runtime.v) and [legacy upload completion](../src/upload_runtime.v) reclaim event results they do not transfer: close an unconsumed connection before finishing its worker lease, including non-response and rejected response paths. Standalone event dispatch still returns its outcome to the caller, which owns its cleanup. These views borrow executor owners and do not delay or override retirement.

Seven new [regression functions](../src/transform_engine_owner_test.v) cover replacement during a transform callback, retained HTTP/pipeline engines, named fallback isolation, retired origin errors, raw/leased stream cleanup, rejected response cleanup and standalone ownership transfer. The final related run passes all 90 permanent functions (seven new) and two original-source probes, which reproduce engine retargeting and an unconsumed stream owner before the fix. The DB-enabled V3 build passes with all 437 production V inputs matching staging; formatting, safety lint and diff checks pass. Complete default E2E passes all 209 CLI/config/runtime checks plus Node/PHP regressions. Source/test/stage hashes were reverified after E2E, and the tested binary is installed (SHA256 `7c5adaf4b57b3604faf06af94fed62b196843c92e757a07293c9cbee4ba18c65`). Linux preflight/final read-only queries each timed out after 15 seconds without VM changes or tests. Evidence is retained in `tmp/transform-engine-owner-validation/report.txt`. Full V-suite/load/live-DB, Linux and wider subsystem audits remain separate follow-ups.

### Discarded dispatch outcome and transferred lease ownership — 2026-10-17

[HTTP rendering](../src/http_response_runtime.v) and [native WebSocket opening](../src/websocket_ingress_runtime.v) now defer unclaimed-outcome cleanup, covering ordinary responses, invalid upstream plans, rejected opens and missing native connections. Successful stream/WebSocket takeover retains cleanup with its actual consumer. [Outcome close semantics](../src/executor/types.v) explicitly document that discarding a copy cannot finish a transferred lease.

[VJSX transforms](../src/vjsx_transformer_runtime.v) and [upload completion](../src/upload_runtime.v) now discard only unclaimed ownership. Removing their unconditional finish_worker call prevents an ignored result copy from ending accounting while another consumer still holds its transferred connection. Existing physical close-before-finish behavior remains for unclaimed results.

### Upstream invocation registration and snapshot ownership — 2026-10-17

[UpstreamRuntimeRegistry](../src/upstream_runtime_registry.v) now returns an explicit optional, opaque per-invocation registration. Client request IDs remain correlation/display fields; records use owner-qualified sequence keys allocated under the registry mutex, so concurrent requests sharing an ID cannot overwrite or clean up one another. [Upstream execution](../src/upstream_runtime.v) retains its accepted registration and releases precisely that token on return. Registry-owned callbacks and [App forwarding](../src/app_upstream_runtime.v) carry the registration rather than a request ID. Empty request IDs retain the existing unregistered behavior via an explicit absent token.

Registry snapshots capture detached session values under the owner lock and sort/page after unlocking. Sort keys retain invocation identity for duplicate request IDs, and nonnegative bounded pagination avoids invalid ranges and positive offset-plus-limit overflow. Existing snapshot JSON/session fields are preserved. This registration tracks the execution lifetime; HTTP/native cancellation and connection ownership remain unchanged.

Seven new [regression functions](../src/upstream_registration_owner_test.v) cover duplicate/cross-registry tokens, 24 concurrent same-ID requests, retained/filterable duplicate rows, paging limits, absent registration and actual concurrent execution failure cleanup with TCP responses. ### Bridge pending RPC registration ownership — 2026-10-17

[Bridge request registration](../src/feishu_card_bridge_runtime.v) now allocates request IDs and publishes result channels under the same [FeishuState](../src/feishu/types.v) mutex. Callback and proxy requests share an owner-local monotonic sequence instead of microsecond wall-clock IDs. A repeated timestamp could overwrite an existing waiter and let older deferred cleanup remove the newer entry. ### Feishu buffer asynchronous commit ownership — 2026-10-17

[Buffer snapshots and commit helpers](../src/feishu/state.v) now capture a private buffer identity under the state-owner mutex. Preview writeback checks identity and prior render/flush state, preserves concurrently appended data as eligible for another flush even within the same millisecond, and rejects completion after sealing or a newer commit. Final writeback checks identity and exact captured content; a changed buffer is preserved and the caller receives a conflict. [Flushing](../src/feishu_stream_buffer_runtime.v) uses these owner helpers, skips sealed buffers, and no longer deletes a buffer merely because a followup send returned the same message ID. ### Feishu rollover send registration and publication ownership — 2026-10-17

[Sealed-buffer successor sends](../src/feishu/buffer_rollover.v) now reserve an explicit identity-bound claim under FeishuState ownership. Concurrent deltas coalesce behind one send; commit checks the root identity/claim, rejects empty or already-owned successor IDs and publishes a single linked successor with all queued content. Failure releases only its claim and preserves text for a later patch/retry; clear/rebind invalidates old claims. Patch traversal rejects cycles, missing linked buffers and foreign stream/app owners. Rebinding or an explicit content reset starts a new lifecycle instead of inheriting old sealed/link/pending state.

[Root publication](../src/feishu_stream_buffer_runtime.v) pairs successor-buffer and Codex-target metadata under the FeishuState lock; [session clear](../src/command_handlers.v) uses the same FeishuState → CodexState order for buffer/target retirement. HTTP and business dispatch callbacks remain outside the lock. Already admitted external sends may complete, but retired claims cannot publish local successors or targets.

### Executor lane warmup timeout: embedded compiler asset decode — 2026-10-17

The previously unconfirmed warmup timeout is traced to loading the 9,112,572-byte embedded TypeScript compiler on each cold lane. V3's gzip implementation expands DEFLATE through generic V byte/array operations; sampled stacks show this work before application-module import. Under constrained scheduling it crosses the existing 10-second maintenance-task deadline. Existing startup behavior logs that error and continues, so listener health alone does not prove executor readiness.

The local vjsx dependency now decodes trusted embedded gzip members with miniz already shipped with V. The native helper validates the gzip header, exact payload consumption, decoded size and CRC32; V owns the output buffer and the returned string has independent storage. Filesystem asset overrides and general gzip APIs retain their existing paths. Debug-only decode timings isolate this stage from JS evaluation. The task deadline and existing startup-error policy are unchanged. Source changes are in `/Users/guweigang/Source/vjsx/runtime_assets.v`, `runtime_asset_gzip.c.v`, `runtime_asset_gzip.h` and `runtime_asset_gzip_test.v`; they remain local/uncommitted and have not been published upstream.

A new [E2E regression](../tests/e2e/executor_lane_warmup_probe.py), included in the default runner, delays only its own child process while embedded decode is active and always resumes that child before cleanup. With identical production sources and phase instrumentation, the old-decoder control hits the original warmup timeout; the native decoder completes both warmups under the same 20 ms runnable / 700 ms paused schedule (8.145 seconds initially, 8.382 seconds in complete E2E). Decoder symbols verify that the control/final binaries use the intended implementations. Standalone same-input comparisons preserve the compiler SHA256 and measure approximately threefold lower decode time on this host; this is not a cross-platform latency guarantee.

Six new decoder tests and ten existing asset/TypeScript lifecycle tests pass, including 64 repeated bootstrap/context-close cycles, asset overrides, malformed members and concurrent independent results. The DB-enabled V3 Boehm build and complete default E2E pass: new delayed-warmup regression, all 209 existing CLI/config/runtime checks and Node/PHP regressions. Format, ownership lint and both repositories' diff checks pass. All 444 frozen vhttpd production inputs and task/dependency manifests match after testing; the tested binary is installed atomically and its help invocation verified (SHA256 `011a7c646d347ffb198d153211d9488be38508937f9358d3d24f452e17b53df5`). Linux read-only preflight passes, but pre-existing tracked edits trigger the Linux skill stop rule; no VM builds/tests. Evidence, dependency patch, before/after snapshots, stack samples, failed diagnostic fixtures and complete E2E artifacts are retained in `tmp/lane-warmup-validation/`.

### Executor lane build-directory ownership — 2026-10-17

Audit found that executors with the same entry, source signature and lane index in one process shared a deterministic build directory. Resetting or closing either executor could recursively remove files still owned by the other.
